# FoodKing SYSTEMS — Massive Read-Only Audit & Correction Report

**Date** : 2026-06-04 · **Scope** : ALL systems beyond the POS box — Admin dashboards, Settings, Catalog, Orders, Reports, CRM, Staff, Delivery, Marketing, KDS, OSS, Kiosk · **Mode** : READ-ONLY (zero code modification).
**Method** : 17 parallel micro-system auditors → adversarial RED challenge (existence + necessity) → main-agent live capture → report self-audit. Companion to `reports/audit/pos-box-massive-2026-06-04/` (the POS box).

## Executive summary

Confirmed corrections (survived adversarial existence + necessity challenge): **90** — P0 **0**, P1 **9**, P2 **29**, P3 **52**.
Dropped by the adversarial pass (hallucinated / NO-OP / works-as-intended): **26**. Confirmed solid: **76**. Coverage gaps flagged by RED: **86**.

> **Owner-relevant (ties to your terminal task):** a TPE registered via Settings as admin can land at `branch_id=0` and be invisible to the branch caisse (S8-01) — assign terminals to the branch (`branch_id=1`). And `S7-03` (admin App-Debug toggle writes `APP_DEBUG=true` to `.env`) is an operational footgun to avoid during the cloud cutover.

## Detailed findings by micro-system

### S1-dashboard — S1-dashboard
Files examined: `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/DashboardComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/OverviewComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/OrderStatisticsComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/SalesSummaryComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/OrderSummaryComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/CustomerStatsComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/RealtimeReportComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/ChannelStatsComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/SlaAlertsComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/LastZReportWidget.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/TopCustomersComponent.vue`, `resources/js/store/modules/dashboard.js`, `resources/js/services/appService.js`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/DashboardController.php`, `app/Services/DashboardService.php`, `app/Models/Order.php`, `app/Http/Resources/SalesSummaryResource.php`, `app/Http/Resources/TopCustomerResource.php`, `app/Libraries/AppLibrary.php`, `routes/api.php`, `tests/Feature/Dashboard/DashboardRevenueNettingSentinelTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Dashboard/SalesSummaryAvgPerDayDivisorSentinelTest.php`

**Confirmed corrections:**

- **[P1] Date-range filters silently broken: datepicker sends raw JS Date toString (GMT+0200 ...) which Carbon cannot parse -> backend 422 -> chart keeps showing the STALE previous range with no error** — `resources/js/services/appService.js:243-263 (requestHandler) + SalesSummaryComponent.vue:88-91 + OrderSummaryComponent.vue:105-110 + OrderStatisticsComponent.vue:186-194 + CustomerStatsComponent.vue:75-80 ; consumed by app/Services/DashboardService.php:135-137 (Carbon::parse($request->first_date))` (logic, code)
  - **What** : The 4 filterable widgets push the datepicker raw Date objects into the Vuex payload: SalesSummaryComponent.vue:89 date.first_date = e[0] (e[0] is a JS Date, picker has utc=false). dashboard.js:156-169 builds the URL via appService.requestHandler(payload). requestHandler (appService.js:253) does naive concat: response += request + '=' + requests[request]. A JS Date coerced by + yields e.g. 'Fri May 01 2026 00:00:00 GMT+0200 (heure ete...)' (reproduced via node String(new Date)). Per WHATWG/PHP a literal + in a query value decodes to a SPACE at PHP input, giving '...GMT 0200...'. DashboardService.php:136 then runs Carbon::parse('...GMT 0200 (...)'). Reproduced with php -r: Carbon throws 'Could not parse ... Double timezone specification' -> service catch rethrows Exception(422), controller returns HTTP 422. Front-end .catch (SalesSummaryComponent.vue:126-128) only sets loading=false; does NOT reset total_sales/options, so the widget keeps the previously-loaded default-month numbers. WHY it survived every GOAL-G2/Wave-T TZ heal: grep tests/ shows every dashboard test passes clean strings (DashboardRevenueNettingSentinelTest.php:81 'first_date'=>'2026-03-01'), bypassing the datepicker the real UI uses.
  - **Why it matters** : This is the owner exact flou: the period filter LOOKS like it works (no error) but selecting Mois dernier / a custom range leaves the chart and KPI cards showing the DEFAULT current-month figures. The owner makes decisions (which day was strong, average ticket) on numbers that do not match the period he picked, with zero signal the request failed. Hits the 4 most-used analytical widgets.
  - **Reproduce** : Open /admin/dashboard. In Resume des ventes (SalesSummary) pick a non-default range (preset Mois dernier). Network: GET admin/dashboard/sales-summary?first_date=<unencoded JS Date string> returns 422 {message:'Double timezone specification ...'}. UI shows no error, chart/KPIs stay on previously-loaded current-month data. Code-proven: node String(new Date) shape + URLSearchParams (+ ->space) + php -r Carbon::parse(mangled) throws.
  - **Correction** : Format dates to Y-m-d before dispatch. (a) in each handler convert e[0] to a local YYYY-MM-DD string, OR (b) harden appService.requestHandler to URL-encode values and convert Date instances to YYYY-MM-DD -- option (b) fixes every caller at once with smallest blast radius. Add a test that feeds a real Date-derived string (with spaces) through the controller; current sentinels only feed clean Y-m-d and miss this.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED REAL P1. Frontend cites are EXACT: SalesSummaryComponent.vue:88-89 (this.first_date=e[0], picker utc=false), OrderSummaryComponent.vue:106-109, OrderStatisticsComponent.vue:186-188, CustomerStatsComponent.vue:76-79 all push the raw JS Date object into the Vuex payload. appService.js:243-263 requestHandler does naive concat (response += request + '=' + requests[request]) with ZERO url-encoding — so 'Fri May 01 2026 00:00:00 GMT+0200 (heure...)' is sent raw. Reproduced node String(new Date) -> exact string. Reproduced php Carbon::parse(decoded,'Europe/Paris') -> THROWS InvalidFormatException 'Double timezone specification' on BOTH realistic decode paths (+ -> space). salesSummary() at DashboardService.php:204-277 has NO try/catch around the Carbon::parse at :211, so it propagates to DashboardController.php:116-117 catch -> HTTP 422. Components' .catch (SalesSummary:126-128 etc) only flips loading=false, does NOT reset displayed KPIs -> stale numbers remain with no error. CAVEAT: the finding's backend cite 'DashboardService.php:135-137 (Carbon::parse($request->first_date))' is IMPRECISE — :135-137 is resolveDayBoundaryParis (used by orderStatistics path); the actual unguarded salesSummary parse is :211 (also :286 customerStates), and the call signature is Carbon::parse($x,$appTz) not bare. Mechanism and 422 outcome fully confirmed regardless. Real owner-facing flou: period filter looks like it works but silently keeps default-month numbers.

- **[P2] Two cards both labelled Total des ventes show different scopes (all-time net vs selected-period net) with no scope hint** — `OverviewComponent.vue:12-13 + app/Services/DashboardService.php:347-356 (totalSales, all-time) vs SalesSummaryComponent.vue:24-26 + app/Services/DashboardService.php:228-234 (salesSummary, date-ranged)` (ux, code)
  - **What** : OverviewComponent.vue:12 renders label.total_sales bound to DashboardService::totalSales() = orderQuery()->realizedRevenue()->sum('total') (DashboardService.php:351) with NO date bound = lifetime CA. SalesSummaryComponent.vue:26 renders the SAME i18n key label.total_sales bound to salesSummary() total_sales which IS date-bounded (DashboardService.php:228-234, default current month). Two on-screen numbers, identical label, very different magnitudes.
  - **Why it matters** : On one screen the owner sees Total des ventes = (e.g.) 250000 EUR in the Overview banner and Total des ventes = 8000 EUR in the Sales-summary card. Same words, 30x apart. This unexplained discrepancy makes the owner distrust the whole dashboard. A one-word qualifier removes it.
  - **Reproduce** : Load /admin/dashboard with >1 month of orders. Compare the Overview Total des ventes tile against the Total des ventes figure inside Resume des ventes. They differ (lifetime vs current month).
  - **Correction** : Relabel: Overview tile -> CA total / Total des ventes (cumul); SalesSummary figure -> CA de la periode (or show the active range under it). No backend change; add distinct i18n keys.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED. OverviewComponent.vue:12 renders {{ $t('label.total_sales') }} bound to total_sales fed by DashboardService::totalSales() = orderQuery()->realizedRevenue()->sum('total') at :347-356 with NO date bound (lifetime CA). SalesSummaryComponent.vue:26 renders the SAME i18n key label.total_sales bound to salesSummary() total_sales which IS date-bounded (DashboardService.php:228-234, default current month). Identical label, two scopes, large magnitude gap. Real UX trust defect; one-word qualifier fixes it. Cosmetic/copy fix, no backend change.

- **[P3] ChannelStats card Repartition par Canal (Aujourdhui) buckets do not partition the order set -> the three percentages can sum to >100 or <100** — `app/Services/DashboardService.php:520-543 (channelStatistics bucketing)` (logic, code)
  - **What** : kioskCount (DashboardService.php:520-526) counts source_surface=='kiosk' OR source==APP. webCount (528-536) correctly EXCLUDES kiosk-tagged rows. BUT posCount (538) is $orders->where('source', Source::POS)->count() with NO exclusion of kiosk-tagged rows, so a kiosk row whose legacy source is POS counts in BOTH kiosk and pos -> sum >100. Also the three buckets are computed independently against $total (483) with no completeness guard: an order whose source is none of WEB/APP/POS and source_surface!=kiosk is counted in NONE -> sum <100. No assertion the buckets partition the set. Contrast eodSynthesis bucketChannels (DashboardService.php:721-737) which classifies each order exactly once and is correct.
  - **Why it matters** : A channel-mix where slices do not sum to 100% is an immediate credibility hit and can mislead the owner about borne vs comptoir share. The kiosk-vs-web bucketing was already re-patched twice (WG-3 WF-3); the POS branch never got the same kiosk-exclusion guard.
  - **Reproduce** : Construct today orders incl. one with source=Source::POS AND source_surface='kiosk'. channelStatistics counts it in kioskCount and again in posCount -> sum of the three percentages >100. ChannelStatsComponent renders each as an independent bar so it is visible.
  - **Correction** : Make the buckets a true partition: classify each order exactly once (kiosk first, then POS only if not kiosk, then web) -- reuse the single-pass bucketChannels logic already correct in eodSynthesis.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED REAL and PROMOTED P2->P1. Code cite exact: channelStatistics buckets are computed INDEPENDENTLY against $total — kioskCount (DashboardService.php:520-526), webCount excludes kiosk (528-536), but posCount=$orders->where('source',Source::POS)->count() (538) with Source::POS=15 and NO legacy-int handling. LIVE GROUP BY source,source_surface DISPROVES the partition: only 54 rows have source=15, but there are 1343 rows source='1' surface='pos' (legacy POS int) + 30 source='1' surface=NULL + 3 source=NULL surface='pos' + source='3','4','mobile','POS'(string) etc. ALL of these ~1400 real POS/other orders fall into NO bucket (source not in {5,10,15} and surface!=kiosk) -> the three percentages sum FAR below 100 (POS shows 54/total instead of ~1400/total). This is not a rare theoretical edge: it is the dominant real data shape — channel mix is grossly wrong TODAY, materially misleading borne-vs-comptoir share. The correct single-pass partition already exists in eodSynthesis bucketChannels (DashboardService.php:716-737, classifies each order once via continue). Promote: live data proves it is actively wrong, not dormant.
  - **Self-audit re-rating** : P1 → P3
  - **Self-audit note** : Self-audit re-rating P1->P3: the Repartition-par-Canal buckets provably do not partition (POS double-counts a kiosk-tagged source=POS row; an order in none of WEB/APP/POS/kiosk counts in none) — REAL, but it is a read-only dashboard percentage with no money/fiscal/blocking impact.

- **[P2] All four date-filtered widgets swallow API errors (.catch only flips loading=false) -> no empty/error state, failures invisible; RealtimeReport has no .catch at all** — `SalesSummaryComponent.vue:126-128 + OrderSummaryComponent.vue:155-157 + OrderStatisticsComponent.vue:206-208,230-232 + CustomerStatsComponent.vue:128-130 + RealtimeReportComponent.vue:45-49` (ux, code)
  - **What** : Every widget promise-rejection handler is .catch((err)=>{ this.loading.isActive=false; }) with no user-facing error state and no reset of displayed values. RealtimeReportComponent.vue:46-48 has NO .catch, so a failed poll throws an unhandled rejection and the 30s interval (line 39) keeps firing. No loading skeleton beyond the global LoadingComponent and no donnees indisponibles state on these cards (only LastZReportWidget.vue:9-11 and ChannelStats empty arrays handle emptiness).
  - **Why it matters** : Combined with S1-DASH-01 this is why the broken date filter is invisible: the API 422s, the catch hides it, stale numbers remain. Even independently, a backend hiccup shows the owner old data dressed as current. An error/empty state turns silent-wrong into visible-degraded, which the owner can trust.
  - **Reproduce** : Force any dashboard endpoint to 500/422 (revoke permission:dashboard). Widgets show no error -- they keep last values or render blank charts with no message. RealtimeReport logs an unhandled promise rejection.
  - **Correction** : Add a per-widget error flag (set in .catch) rendering Donnees indisponibles, reessayer, and reset displayed KPI values on failure so stale numbers are not presented as live. Add a .catch to RealtimeReportComponent.fetchData.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED, and UNDER-SCOPED by the original finding. All four date widgets' .catch only flips loading.isActive=false with no error/empty state and no reset of displayed values: SalesSummaryComponent.vue:126-128, OrderSummaryComponent.vue:155-157, OrderStatisticsComponent.vue:205-207 & 230-232, CustomerStatsComponent.vue:128-130. RealtimeReportComponent.vue:45-49 fetchData has NO .catch and polls every 30s (setInterval :39) -> unhandled rejection loop. ADDITIONAL (verifier-found, finding missed it): SlaAlertsComponent.vue fetchData ALSO has NO .catch and polls every 15s (setInterval) -> same unhandled-rejection bug. This is the mechanism that makes DASH-01 invisible. Real degraded-vs-wrong defect.

- **[P3] customerStates fires 18 separate queries per load and hydrates full Order models just to count (->get()->count())** — `app/Services/DashboardService.php:301-316` (perf, code)
  - **What** : The loop runs once per hour bucket (18 iterations, DashboardService.php:295 timeArray) and each iteration executes (clone $order)->where(...)->whereTime(...)->whereTime(...)->get()->count() (DashboardService.php:309-314). ->get()->count() SELECTs and hydrates every matching Order into memory then counts the collection, instead of ->count() which issues a single COUNT(*). On a busy month that is 18 full range hydrations on every dashboard load + datepicker change.
  - **Why it matters** : On V1-local single box the dataset is modest so impact is bounded, but this widget re-runs on every date change and the hydrate-to-count x18 pattern is needless DB + memory churn growing linearly with order volume. Trivial fix, real waste.
  - **Reproduce** : Enable query log, load /admin/dashboard. customerStates emits 18 SELECT * queries (one per hour bucket) each materialising rows before counting, versus the 1-COUNT-each it should.
  - **Correction** : Replace ->get()->count() with ->count() (single COUNT(*) per bucket), or better collapse all 18 buckets into one GROUP BY hour query so the whole widget is a single round-trip.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED. customerStates loops 18 times (timeArray :295 has 18 hour labels) and each iteration runs (clone $order)->where(...)->whereTime(...)->whereTime(...)->get()->count() at DashboardService.php:309-314. ->get()->count() hydrates every matching Order into memory then counts the collection instead of a single COUNT(*). Real waste (18 full hydrations per dashboard load AND per datepicker change), grows linearly with volume. Trivial fix (->count() or single GROUP BY HOUR). P3 correct — bounded on V1 single box but genuine.

**Dropped by adversarial pass:**
- ~~[P2] CustomerStats hour-of-day chart buckets by Paris-local hour but whereTime runs on a TIMESTAMP in MySQL session tz -> known-wrong by 2h, shipped to owner as Statistiques clients~~ — DROP — REAL-IN-THEORY but DORMANT/FALSE in actual V1 single-box config; a hostile verifier must not accept the self-incriminating comment as proof. The comment at DashboardService.php:304-308 claims whereTime surfaces time 'in MySQL session UTC' -> 2h offset. VERIFIED LIVE: app.timezone=Europe/Paris; DB connection timezone=null (not set); order_datetime is a MySQL TIMESTAMP column (2022_11_17_110810_create_orders_table.php:31); @@session.time_zone=SYSTEM (consistent for both write and read). Live query of 5 recent orders: order_datetime EQUALS created_at and HOUR(order_datetime) returns the Paris wall-clock hour (15:46->15, 12:20->12, 06:02->6). Because the session tz is identical on write and read, the TIMESTAMP round-trips to the same wall-clock value Laravel wrote (Paris-local), so whereTime hour == Paris axis label hour == correct. No 2h offset is observable on this single-box deployment. The claimed staffing-misread does NOT occur. (It would only bite if write-tz != read-tz, e.g. a multi-instance/cloud cutover — that is the V1.0.2/cloud backlog scenario, not V1 LOCAL.) is_real=false for the V1 deployment.
- ~~[P3] TopCustomers ranks by LIFETIME order count ignoring the dashboard date filters -> top clients never reflects the period the owner is looking at~~ — DROP — backend cite accurate but the claimed OWNER-FACING harm cannot occur: the widget is DEAD CODE. topCustomers() at DashboardService.php:324-345 is indeed lifetime-scoped (no date bound) as claimed. BUT VERIFIED: TopCustomersComponent.vue is imported/mounted in ZERO .vue templates (grep across resources/js found no usage outside the component's own file); DashboardComponent.vue mounts FeaturedItemsComponent + MostPopularItemsComponent instead. The store action dashboard/topCustomers (dashboard.js:212) and route admin/dashboard/top-customers exist but are never dispatched. The finding's reproduction ('TopCustomers does not change because it is lifetime') is impossible — the card is not on the dashboard the owner sees. No user-facing scope blur. is_real=false (no live defect surfaced). Dead-code cleanup is a separate, non-defect concern.

### S2-orders-mgmt — S2-orders-mgmt
Files examined: `resources/js/components/admin/onlineOrders/OnlineOrderListComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/onlineOrders/OnlineOrderShowComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/posOrders/PosOrderListComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/posOrders/PosOrderShowComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/orderHistory/HistoriqueListComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/transactions/TransactionListComponent.vue`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OnlineOrderController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/PosOrderController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderHistoryController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/TransactionController.php`, `app/Services/OrderService.php`, `app/Domain/Order/OrderStateMachine.php`, `app/Domain/Order/PaymentStateMachine.php`, `app/Rules/ValidStatusTransition.php`, `app/Http/Requests/OrderStatusRequest.php`, `app/Http/Resources/SimpleOrderResource.php`, `app/Libraries/AppLibrary.php`, `routes/api.php`

**Confirmed corrections:**

- **[P2] POS order detail: payment-status dropdown offers an option (Impayé) that the backend ALWAYS rejects — every default POS order is created PAID and PaymentStateMachine has no PAID->UNPAID edge** — `resources/js/components/admin/posOrders/PosOrderShowComponent.vue:577-582 (paymentStatusObject) + :139-150 (dropdown) ; app/Domain/Order/PaymentStateMachine.php:17 (PAID => []) ; app/Services/OrderService.php:783 (payment_status => PaymentStatus::PAID at POS creation)` (logic, code)
  - **What** : paymentStatusObject() returns BOTH {Payé, PAID} and {Impayé, UNPAID} unconditionally. The dropdown @click='changePaymentStatus(paymentStatus.value)' POSTs to changePaymentStatus -> OrderService::changePaymentStatus -> PaymentStateMachine::assertCanTransition. PaymentStateMachine::TRANSITIONS defines PaymentStatus::PAID => [] (line 17), so PAID->UNPAID throws InvalidArgumentException('Invalid payment_status transition 5 -> 10', 422). Default inline POS orders are persisted with 'payment_status' => PaymentStatus::PAID (OrderService.php:783). Therefore on the overwhelming majority of POS orders the 'Impayé' menu entry is a live, clickable control that can only ever produce a red 422 error toast; clicking 'Payé' on a PAID order is a silent no-op early-return (changePaymentStatus.php:2350). The dropdown is effectively dead for its visible default state.
  - **Why it matters** : Owner explicitly flagged 'flous / unnecessary things that are not good'. A cashier opening a normal paid POS order sees a payment dropdown whose only non-current option (Impayé) throws an error every single time. It looks like a broken feature on a money-bearing control, erodes trust during a live shift, and trains staff to ignore error toasts. There is no legitimate PAID->UNPAID path in V1, so the option should not be offered.
  - **Reproduce** : POS detail of any normally-paid POS order -> click the payment-status dropdown -> choose 'Impayé' -> 422 toast 'Invalid payment_status transition 5 -> 10' (message surfaced via PosOrderController::changePaymentStatus catch -> err.response.data.message). Re-select 'Payé' -> nothing happens (no-op).
  - **Correction** : Make paymentStatusObject status-aware like a transition list: when order.payment_status === PAID, render no actionable options (or a disabled/read-only badge); only when payment_status is UNPAID/PENDING_COUNTER expose the {PAID} option. Mirror the state machine (PaymentStateMachine::TRANSITIONS) so the UI never offers an edge the backend forbids.
  - **RED verdict** : Confirmed full chain. PosOrderShowComponent.vue:577-582 paymentStatusObject returns BOTH {PAID, UNPAID} unconditionally; dropdown :143 wires @click=changePaymentStatus. PaymentStateMachine.php:17 PaymentStatus::PAID => [] so PAID->UNPAID throws InvalidArgumentException(422) at :32. OrderService.php:783 sets payment_status=PAID for default POS orders (unless deferToCounter). changePaymentStatus routes through assertCanTransition at OrderService.php:2384/2417, and the no-op early-return is at :2350-2351. So on a normal paid POS order the only non-current option (Impayé) always 422s. REAL UX defect on a money control. Downgrade P1->P2: backend is correct/safe (no fiscal or data risk), the no-op/422 are contained, and impact is a confusing-but-harmless dropdown option, not a broken flow.

- **[P2] Order-status dropdown (POS + Online detail) lists ALL forward statuses regardless of current state — illegal/backward transitions are offered as clickable and fail with a 422 toast instead of being hidden** — `resources/js/components/admin/posOrders/PosOrderShowComponent.vue:535-566 (orderStatusObject) + :159-170 ; resources/js/components/admin/onlineOrders/OnlineOrderShowComponent.vue:370-398 + :145-162 ; guarded by app/Domain/Order/OrderStateMachine.php:30-92 (allows())` (logic, code)
  - **What** : orderStatusObject() returns a FLAT list [ACCEPT, PREPARING, PREPARED, (OUT_FOR_DELIVERY), DELIVERED] independent of order.status. Each @click='orderStatus(status.value)' POSTs change-status -> OrderService::changeStatus -> ValidStatusTransition -> OrderStateMachine::allows(). allows() only permits a forward ladder (e.g. PREPARING -> {PREPARED, CANCELED} or DELIVERED w/ pos perm; PREPARED -> {OUT_FOR_DELIVERY, DELIVERED}; DELIVERED -> {RETURNED only}). So selecting e.g. 'Accepté' on an order already PREPARED, or any backward move, hits 'invalid_status_transition' (422) — the dropdown shows the current status highlighted but still lets you pick illegal earlier/forward states.
  - **Why it matters** : This is the core 'blur' on the most-used action of the screen during service. Staff see 5 status choices on every order with no indication of which are reachable; picking the wrong one yields a generic error toast rather than the UI simply not offering it. Wastes time at the counter, looks buggy, and makes the legitimate path (advance one step) harder to find. The backend is correct (safe), but the UX leaks the state machine through error toasts.
  - **Reproduce** : Open a POS order whose status = Prepared. Open the status dropdown -> pick 'Accepté' or 'En préparation' -> toast 'invalid_status_transition'. Only PREPARED -> Livré / Out-for-delivery actually succeed.
  - **Correction** : Derive orderStatusObject from OrderStateMachine::allows() for the current order.status (expose an allowed-next-states list via the resource, or replicate the small transition table client-side). Render only the legal next states (advance-one-step + permitted terminals); disable/grey the rest. Keep the full enum only for the display label map.
  - **RED verdict** : Confirmed. PosOrderShowComponent.vue:553-565 orderStatusObject returns a FLAT forward list [ACCEPT,PREPARING,PREPARED,(OUT_FOR_DELIVERY),DELIVERED] independent of order.status; OnlineOrderShowComponent.vue:385-397 identical. Dropdown :163 wires orderStatus(value) -> change-status -> OrderService.php:2017 ValidStatusTransition.php:35 -> OrderStateMachine::allows() (only a forward ladder; backward/earlier states return false). So picking e.g. ACCEPT on a PREPARED order yields invalid_status_transition (422). REAL: the UI leaks the state machine through error toasts on the most-used action. Backend is correct/safe. Downgrade P1->P2: no data/fiscal risk, only counter-time waste + buggy appearance; same class/impact as S2-01.

- **[P2] Unified Historique status filter cannot find Annulé / Rejeté / Retourné orders — dropdown omits all terminal states while the page advertises full history + a REFUNDED payment filter** — `resources/js/components/admin/orderHistory/HistoriqueListComponent.vue:33-40 (status options) vs :44-52 (payment offers REFUNDED) ; backend exact-match at app/Services/OrderService.php:172-173 ($query->where('status',(int)$request))` (logic, code)
  - **What** : The Historique status <vue-select> offers exactly [ACCEPT, PREPARING, PREPARED, DELIVERED]. It omits CANCELED(16), REJECTED(19), RETURNED(22). OrderService::list applies status as an EXACT where (line 172-173), so there is no way to filter the unified history to cancelled/rejected/refunded orders by status. Yet the same form's PAYMENT filter does offer REFUNDED (line 49), and the table renders a refund tag (↩ #parent) + a Retourné status label (enums.orderStatusEnumArray includes RETURNED at :220). The page header/menu is 'Historique' (see one-page-see-everything mandate in OrderHistoryController docblock).
  - **Why it matters** : Owner mandate for /admin/historique is 'see EVERYTHING from one page'. A manager reconciling the day cannot isolate cancelled or refunded orders by status on the one screen built for that — the most audit-relevant rows (cancellations, returns/refunds) are unreachable via the status filter. Inconsistent with the payment filter exposing REFUNDED, so the UI implies these orders are filterable when they are not.
  - **Reproduce** : /admin/historique -> Filtres -> Statut dropdown shows only Accepté/En préparation/Préparé/Livré. There is no Annulé/Rejeté/Retourné entry, so a refunded (Retourné) order can never be status-filtered even though it appears in the list with a Retourné badge.
  - **Correction** : Add CANCELED, REJECTED, RETURNED to the Historique status options array (they already exist in orderStatusEnum and the orderStatusClass helper). No backend change needed (exact-match already supports them).
  - **RED verdict** : Confirmed. HistoriqueListComponent.vue:33-38 status options = [ACCEPT(4),PREPARING(7),PREPARED(8),DELIVERED(13)] only; CANCELED(16)/REJECTED(19)/RETURNED(22) omitted. Payment filter :45-50 DOES offer REFUNDED, and the table renders a refund tag :116-118 + Retourné label. Backend OrderService.php:172-173 applies status as exact where((int)$request), so it already supports the missing terminal statuses; pure frontend options array gap, no backend change needed. Inconsistent with the 'see everything from one page' Historique mandate. P2 stands.

- **[P2] Online-orders list 'Montant' column renders raw '19.00' (US format, no € symbol) while POS list + Historique render canonical '19,00 €' — money column formatting diverges across the three order screens** — `resources/js/components/admin/onlineOrders/OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:119 ({{ order.total_amount_price }}) vs resources/js/components/admin/posOrders/PosOrderListComponent.vue:126 ({{ formatPrice(order.total) }}) ; app/Libraries/AppLibrary.php:306-308 (flatAmountFormat = number_format(amount, decimals, '.', '') )` (visual, code)
  - **What** : OnlineOrderList binds order.total_amount_price for the amount cell. SimpleOrderResource maps total_amount_price => AppLibrary::flatAmountFormat($this->total) (SimpleOrderResource.php), and flatAmountFormat returns number_format(amount, CURRENCY_DECIMAL_POINT, '.', '') = e.g. '19.00' — dot decimal, NO currency symbol, NO thousands sep. The sibling PosOrderListComponent (:126) and HistoriqueListComponent (:111) were migrated by the WT-D-R1-F4 heal to formatPrice(order.total) -> '19,00 €'. The raw numeric total is already shipped by SimpleOrderResource (total => round(float)), so the data for the canonical render is present and unused here.
  - **Why it matters** : Same caisse, three different money formats for the same value across Online / POS / Historique. On a French NF525 deployment the amount column on the online-orders screen shows '19.00' with no euro sign — looks unfinished/foreign and is inconsistent with every other order surface that was already canonicalised. Pure formatting, no fiscal impact, but it is exactly the kind of visible inconsistency the owner notices.
  - **Reproduce** : Open /admin/online-orders -> Montant column shows e.g. '19.00'. Open /admin/pos-orders or /admin/historique -> same-priced order shows '19,00 €'.
  - **Correction** : In OnlineOrderListComponent, import adminPriceMixin (already used by PosOrderList/Historique) and render {{ formatPrice(order.total) }} instead of {{ order.total_amount_price }} (total is already exposed raw by SimpleOrderResource).
  - **RED verdict** : Confirmed. OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:119 binds {{ order.total_amount_price }}. SimpleOrderResource.php:49 maps total_amount_price => AppLibrary::flatAmountFormat => number_format(amount, decimals, '.', '') (AppLibrary.php:306-308) = e.g. '19.00', dot decimal, NO euro symbol. Sibling PosOrderListComponent.vue:126 and HistoriqueListComponent.vue:111 render formatPrice(order.total) => '19,00 EUR'. SimpleOrderResource.php:42 already ships raw numeric total (round((float)$this->total,2)), so the canonical data is present and unused. Real visible FR/NF525 inconsistency, trivial one-line fix. Auditor labeled visual; P2 stands (cross-surface money-format divergence on a fiscal product is more than P3 cosmetic).

- **[P3] Online-orders list 'Avance' badge is styled with the order-status colour helper fed a boolean enum, so it renders as a RED (cancelled/error-coloured) pill instead of an informative tag** — `resources/js/components/admin/onlineOrders/OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:127-130 (:class="orderStatusClass(order.is_advance_order)") ; helper resources/js/services/appService.js orderStatusClass() else-branch -> red ; isAdvanceOrderEnum.YES = 5` (visual, code)
  - **What** : The advance badge does :class='orderStatusClass(order.is_advance_order)' where is_advance_order === isAdvanceOrderEnum.YES (5). orderStatusClass() matches on orderStatusEnum values (ACCEPT=4, PREPARING=7, ...); 5 matches none, so it falls into the final else branch which returns the RED pill (text-red-900 bg-red-100) — the same class used for CANCELED/REJECTED. The 'Avance' (advance/scheduled order) marker thus looks like an error/cancelled state.
  - **Why it matters** : Misleading colour semantics: a perfectly normal advance/scheduled online order gets a red 'danger' badge next to its status, which on a glance reads like a problem order. It is a misuse of a status-colour helper on a non-status boolean. Low impact (cosmetic), but it is a genuine logic/visual smell on the online list.
  - **Reproduce** : Place/seed an advance (scheduled) online order, open /admin/online-orders -> the 'Avance' chip in the Statut column is red like a cancelled order.
  - **Correction** : Use a dedicated neutral/info class for the advance marker (e.g. an amber/blue 'Avance' chip) rather than passing a boolean enum to orderStatusClass(). The status pill itself already uses orderStatusClass(order.status) correctly on the adjacent span.
  - **RED verdict** : Both layers confirmed. (1) OnlineOrderController.php:48 feeds the online list via SimpleOrderResource::collection; grep confirms SimpleOrderResource.php has NO is_advance_order key (it exists only in OrderResource/OrderDetailsResource/UserOrderResource/KDSOrderDetailsResource/SimpleDeliveryBoyOrderResource). So order.is_advance_order is undefined on every online-list row; the v-if at OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:127-128 (=== isAdvanceOrderEnum.YES=5) is never true -> Avance chip is dead. (2) :class=orderStatusClass(order.is_advance_order) would pass 5 (YES) into appService.orderStatusClass (appService.js:206); orderStatusEnum has ACCEPT=4/PREPARING=7/PREPARED=8/OUT=10/DELIVERED=13/PENDING=1 (none=5), so 5 falls into the else -> text-red-900 bg-red-100 (red danger pill). Genuine dead/mis-styled element. Low impact (advance online orders are edge-case); P3 stands.

- **[P3] Transactions list date-range presets are hardcoded English ('Today', 'This month', 'This year (slot)') and the datepicker lacks the aria-label present on the other order screens** — `resources/js/components/admin/transactions/TransactionListComponent.vue:186-199 (preset labels) + :58-60 (Datepicker, no :aria-labels) vs OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:214-227 (French presets) + :64-66 (aria-labels)` (ux, code)
  - **What** : TransactionListComponent presetRanges labels are literal English strings: 'Today', 'This month', 'Last month', 'This year', 'This year (slot)'. Every sibling order list (OnlineOrderList:214-227, PosOrderList:216-229, Historique:199-205) uses French labels ('Aujourd'hui', 'Ce mois', 'Mois dernier', 'Cette année'). Additionally the Transactions <Datepicker> (:58) omits the :aria-labels='{ input: $t(...) }' that the other lists pass for a11y.
  - **Why it matters** : FR locale is an immutable V1 mandate (ADR-007). The Transactions screen shows English-only date presets in an otherwise fully-French back office — inconsistent and unprofessional on a French fiscal product. The 'This year (slot)' label even leaks an internal implementation word ('slot'). a11y label gap is a minor regression vs the other three lists.
  - **Reproduce** : /admin/transactions -> open the date filter -> preset chips read 'Today / This month / Last month / This year / This year (slot)' in English, unlike every other order list which reads French.
  - **Correction** : Translate the Transactions preset labels to French (or i18n keys) matching the sibling lists, drop the '(slot)' debug suffix, and add :aria-labels='{ input: $t("label.date") }' to the Datepicker for parity.
  - **RED verdict** : Confirmed. TransactionListComponent.vue:187-198 preset labels are literal English ('Today','This month','Last month','This year','This year (slot)') and the Datepicker :58-60 has no :aria-labels. Sibling lists use French + aria-labels: OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:215-223 ('Aujourd hui','Ce mois','Mois dernier','Cette annee') + :66 :aria-labels; PosOrderListComponent.vue:217-225 + :60 :aria-labels; HistoriqueListComponent.vue:59 :aria-labels. FR locale is an ADR-007 immutable mandate, and '(slot)' leaks an internal slot-name. Cosmetic/i18n, P3 stands.

- **[P3] Online-orders list clear() resets filters to a state that differs from the initial mount filters (excepts and exceptSource drift), so 'Effacer' does not restore the pristine view** — `resources/js/components/admin/onlineOrders/OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:277-278 (mount: excepts=DINING_TABLE, exceptSource=POS) vs :345-356 (clear(): excepts=POS|DINING_TABLE, exceptSource left untouched, never reset)` (logic, code)
  - **What** : Initial search payload (data()) sets excepts: orderTypeEnum.DINING_TABLE (20) and exceptSource: SourceEnum.POS (15). clear() instead sets this.props.search.excepts = orderTypeEnum.POS + '|' + orderTypeEnum.DINING_TABLE ('15|20') and never resets exceptSource (it persists). So after Effacer the list excludes POS via BOTH order_type!=15 AND source!=15 plus order_type!=20 — a strictly different filter expression than on mount. The observable result set is similar (POS still excluded) but the filter state is inconsistent and the code reveals confusion about which signal excludes POS.
  - **Why it matters** : Low user-visible impact (POS stays excluded either way) but it is a real logic blur: the Clear button does not return the screen to its mount state, and two different exclusion mechanisms are mixed. It is the kind of 'unnecessary / not-clean' inconsistency the owner called out, and it is a maintenance trap (future change to one path won't match the other).
  - **Reproduce** : Inspect props.search after mount vs after clicking 'Effacer' on /admin/online-orders: excepts goes from '20' to '15|20', exceptSource stays '15'. Filter expression differs.
  - **Correction** : Make clear() reset to the exact mount defaults: excepts = orderTypeEnum.DINING_TABLE and re-assert exceptSource = SourceEnum.POS (or pick ONE consistent exclusion signal). Align mount + clear so 'Effacer' restores the pristine view.
  - **RED verdict** : Confirmed precisely. Mount data() OnlineOrderListComponent.vue:277 excepts: orderTypeEnum.DINING_TABLE (20), :278 exceptSource: SourceEnum.POS (15). clear() :351 sets excepts = orderTypeEnum.POS + '|' + orderTypeEnum.DINING_TABLE ('15|20') and does NOT reset exceptSource (it persists as '15'; no exceptSource line in clear lines 346-355). So Effacer does not restore the pristine mount filter expression and mixes two POS-exclusion signals. Observable result set is similar (POS stays excluded both ways) so user-visible impact is minimal — but a real logic inconsistency / maintenance trap. P3 + medium confidence appropriate; stands.

**Coverage gaps (RED):**
- confirmCashPayment() (PosOrderShowComponent) — the NF525 counter cash-collection mutation that flips PENDING_COUNTER->PAID was NOT examined; far higher money/fiscal impact than the P3 formatting findings.
- openRefundModal / PosRefundModal refund-with-counter-entry flow + the quick changeStatus(ACCEPT) accept button on PosOrderShowComponent were not audited — the legitimate refund path that S2-01/S2-02 point users toward is itself unverified here.
- destroy(order.id) delete action present on order list/detail — no audit of permission gating, branch isolation, or NF525 implications of deleting a fiscal order.
- selectDeliveryBoy(deliveryBoy.id) assignment mutation (POS + Online detail) — state/branch-isolation correctness unexamined.
- OnlineOrderReasonComponent cancel/reject reason modal (reasonModal) — the CANCELED/REJECTED path with required reason (OrderStateMachine::requiresReason) and its 422 behavior was not examined.
- loyaltyRedeemOpen redemption control on POS order detail — money-adjacent, unaudited.
- Export/PDF/Excel actions on all four lists (ExportComponent/PdfComponent/ExcelComponent) — no check that exported money columns use canonical FR format, which is the very inconsistency S2-04 flags.

### S3-items-catalog — S3-items-catalog
Files examined: `resources/js/components/admin/items/CatalogStudioComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/items/ItemListComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/items/ItemCreateComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/items/composer/ProductComposerEditorComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/items/variation/ItemVariationListComponent.vue`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ItemController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ComposerProfileController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ComposerStepController.php`, `app/Services/ItemService.php`, `app/Services/Composer/ComposerProfileService.php`, `app/Services/Composer/ComposerStepService.php`, `app/Services/Composer/ComposerProfileProjection.php`, `app/Services/Kiosk/KioskMenuService.php`, `app/Services/Menu/MenuProjectionService.php`, `app/Services/Pricing/PricingService.php`, `app/Http/Requests/ItemRequest.php`, `public/js/pos-wizard.js`, `config/catalog_v15.php`, `routes/api.php`

**Confirmed corrections:**

- **[P3] Item update with only NEW variations orphans the old ones (variations diff never runs when no id present)** — `app/Services/ItemService.php:314-349 (guard at 338)` (logic, code)
  - **What** : In ItemService::update the variations sync builds $variationIdsArray only from rows that carry an 'id' (line 322-336). The stale-deletion loop is gated by `if ($variationIdsArray) {` at line 338. If the submitted `variations` JSON contains ONLY new rows (no 'id' keys), $variationIdsArray stays []=falsy, so $variationDeleteArray is never populated and `ItemVariation::whereIn('id',$variationDeleteArray)->delete()` (line 346) never runs. The old DB variations survive alongside the freshly created ones. Proof of intent vs defect: the EXTRAS block right below (lines 352-383) always diff-deletes via `whereNotIn($extraIdsToKeep)` (line 380-382) and pushes both updated and new ids into the keep-set (367,376) — variations do neither. Reachability: the live admin catalog editor (ItemCreateComponent.vue:374-397) does NOT post a `variations` field — variations are CRUDed one-by-one through ItemVariationController (routes/api.php:734-738) — and ItemImport does not route through this path, so this is latent backend SSOT fragility reachable via direct API or any future bulk caller, not a current-UI regression.
  - **Why it matters** : Variations carry their own price (each ItemVariation has name+price). Orphaned old variations re-surface on POS/kiosk/web menu as duplicate or stale-priced size options, which is exactly an SSOT-pricing hazard: a customer could pick a variation the admin believed was removed/repriced, charging the wrong amount on a real shift. The asymmetry with the correct extras path makes it a clear latent defect waiting for the first non-UI caller.
  - **Reproduce** : Create an item with variations V1,V2 (ids 10,11). PUT /api/admin/item/{id} with variations=[{name:'Petit',price:5},{name:'Grand',price:8}] (NO id fields). Expect: only the 2 new variations remain. Actual: V1,V2 (10,11) persist plus the 2 new rows = 4 variations on the item.
  - **Correction** : Mirror the extras diff pattern: collect ALL surviving ids (both updated-with-id AND newly-created ids) into $variationIdsArray, then unconditionally delete `whereNotIn('item_id'=$item->id, 'id', $variationIdsArray)`. Drop the `if ($variationIdsArray)` gate so an all-new payload still removes the old rows.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED ItemService.php:338 `if ($variationIdsArray)` gates the stale-delete loop; an all-new variations payload (no 'id') leaves $variationIdsArray=[] so old rows survive. Extras path (376-382) unconditionally whereNotIn-deletes -> real asymmetry/defect. BUT reachability is latent: ItemCreateComponent.vue posts NO 'variations' blob (grep empty), live edits go through dedicated ItemVariationController (routes/api.php:734-739) one-by-one, store() uses createMany and destroy() deletes all -> only the update-blob path is buggy and only a direct-API/future-bulk caller reaches it. DOWNGRADE P2->P3: identical reachability gate as X1; on a V1 LOCAL single-box where the live editor never hits this path there is no current mischarge. Worth fixing for SSOT robustness.

- **[P2] Reordering steps on a PUBLISHED composer profile leaves editor version stale -> spurious 409 conflict on next save/publish** — `resources/js/components/admin/items/composer/ProductComposerEditorComponent.vue:737-745 + app/Services/Composer/ComposerStepService.php:109-118` (logic, code)
  - **What** : onStepsReordered (editor 737-745) persists a reorder by firing one `axios.patch(admin/composer/steps/{id})` per step and `await Promise.all(requests)` — it never re-hydrates the profile and never refreshes `this.version`. On the backend, ComposerStepService::update -> dispatchProfileChanged (109-118) increments the profile version by 1 and broadcasts ComposerProfileChanged for EACH patched step WHEN the profile is published (guard line 111: `if (!$emitSync || !$profile || !$profile->is_published) return;`). So an N-step drag bumps the server version by N. The editor's only version setter is hydrateProfile (sets this.version = profile.version, confirmed sole setter); there is NO Echo/ComposerProfileChanged listener in the component (grep for Echo|listen|ComposerProfileChanged returned nothing). saveDraft/publish send `version: this.version` (saveDraft line 803) which ComposerProfileService::assertVersionMatches (266-275) compares and aborts 409 on mismatch.
  - **Why it matters** : Real admin trap on a published wizard (the live state on POS+kiosk): drag to reorder a page, then click Save/Publish -> 409 'Profile version conflict' banner forces a reload (reloadProfile), discarding any unsaved field edits the admin made in the same session. It makes the composer feel broken precisely on the items that are already serving customers, and silently lost edits are a data-quality risk.
  - **Reproduce** : Open a PUBLISHED composer profile (admin/composer/items/{id}). Drag-reorder two steps (fires PATCHes, server version goes from v to v+2). Without leaving, edit a step label and click 'Sauvegarder le brouillon'. Expect: save succeeds. Actual: 409, conflict banner appears, edits not saved until reload.
  - **Correction** : After the Promise.all in onStepsReordered, re-hydrate from the last PATCH response (it returns the fresh profile/version) or call reloadProfile-without-resetting-edits; simplest: have the step PATCH responses return the profile version and set this.version to the max returned. Alternatively route reorder through saveDraft (single profile PUT) so only one version bump occurs and the editor re-hydrates via hydrateProfile.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED. onStepsReordered (ProductComposerEditorComponent.vue:737-746) fires per-step axios.patch(admin/composer/steps/{id}) via Promise.all, never re-hydrates, never updates this.version (sole setters lines 622/653 are hydrateProfile only; no Echo listener exists - grep empty). Backend dispatchProfileChanged (ComposerStepService.php:109-118) bumps version+1 per patched step WHEN published (guard 111 `!$profile->is_published`). saveDraft sends version:this.version (803); assertVersionMatches (ComposerProfileService.php:266-274) aborts 409 on mismatch. So an N-step reorder on a PUBLISHED profile -> stale editor version -> guaranteed 409 + lost unsaved edits on next save. Genuinely live-reachable on the exact profiles serving POS/kiosk. Strongest finding; keep P2.

- **[P3] ComposerProfileProjection emits inactive steps (no is_active filter) — defense-in-depth only; every live caller pre-filters** — `app/Services/Composer/ComposerProfileProjection.php:29-48` (logic, code)
  - **What** : project() filters steps only by stepVisibleOn (line 30) and never by is_active, yet it copies is_active into the payload (line 44). In isolation an inactive step would be projected. HOWEVER both real runtime callers pre-filter the steps relation to active: KioskMenuService::publishedComposerProfiles loads `with(['steps' => fn($q)=>$q->where('is_active',true)->orderBy('position')])` (KioskMenuService.php:434) and MenuProjectionService does the same (MenuProjectionService.php:295). PricingService also loads only active steps (PricingService.php:565). So no current caller ever feeds an inactive step to the projection. Consumers that would render an unfiltered payload — pos-wizard.js buildStepsFromComposerProfile (495-535) and normalizeComposerStep (477-490) — drop neither carry is_active, and the kiosk Vue filters client-side (KioskWizardComponent.vue:781). The leak is therefore latent, contingent on a future caller loading steps unfiltered.
  - **Why it matters** : Not a live defect today, but a fragile SSOT seam: the is_active filter lives in 3 separate caller load-clauses instead of in the projection (the single point that builds the runtime contract). A new consumer that loads $profile->steps without the where('is_active',true) clause would silently render deactivated steps on POS/web (the POS wizard has no is_active guard), causing a divergence with PricingService which DOES drop them — operator sees a page the backend won't validate.
  - **Reproduce** : Static: ComposerProfileProjection::project(profile-with-inactive-step) returns that step in 'steps'. No live repro path exists with current callers (all pre-filter).
  - **Correction** : Add `->reject(fn($s)=>!$s->is_active)` (or `->where('is_active', ...)`) inside project() at line 29-30 so the projection is self-consistent regardless of how the caller loaded the relation; keep the caller-side filters as a fast path.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED ComposerProfileProjection.php:29-30 filters steps only by stepVisibleOn and copies is_active at line 44 without filtering it. All 3 live callers pre-filter is_active=true: PricingService.php:565, app/Services/Kiosk/KioskMenuService.php:434, app/Services/Menu/MenuProjectionService.php:295 (finding's paths omitted the Kiosk/ and Menu/ subdirs but line numbers + content match exactly). So no live leak today - defense-in-depth only. Correct P3: a future consumer loading $profile->steps unfiltered would render deactivated steps while PricingService drops them. Self-consistency fix (reject inactive in project()) is sound but non-urgent.

- **[P3] Catalog Studio quick-create hardcodes item_type = VEG for every product (chicken/beef saved as vegetarian)** — `resources/js/components/admin/items/CatalogStudioComponent.vue:488` (logic, code)
  - **What** : buildQuickProductPayload appends `fd.append('item_type', String(itemTypeEnum.VEG))` (line 488) with no UI control to choose veg/non-veg — every product created from the Catalog Studio quick form is classified VEG (enum value 5; NON_VEG=10 per app/Enums/ItemType.php). item_type drives a frontend veg/non-veg filter (resources/js/components/frontend/components/ItemComponent.vue:3 `v-show=type===item.item_type`). NOTE this mirrors the existing full editor default (ItemCreateComponent.vue:337 also defaults item_type:VEG), so it is a pre-existing convention, not a regression introduced by the quick form.
  - **Why it matters** : Data-quality blur the owner flagged ('unnecessary things / not good'): a quick-created Chicken sandwich is tagged vegetarian until someone edits it in the full form, so any veg/non-veg menu filter shows meat items as veg. Low blast radius in V1 Le Cayenne (dietary truth is carried by is_vegetarian/is_halal/allergen_flags booleans, not this legacy item_type), hence P3 — but it is a silent misclassification.
  - **Reproduce** : Catalog Studio -> '+ Produit' quick form -> create 'Chicken Burger'. Inspect items table: item_type = 5 (VEG). Frontend veg filter will list it.
  - **Correction** : Either expose a veg/non-veg toggle in the quick form, or default to NON_VEG (safer for a meat-forward menu), or drop item_type from the quick payload and let the backend default apply. Best: align with the menu reality and remove the silent VEG assumption.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED CatalogStudioComponent.vue:488 hardcodes item_type=VEG(5) for every quick-created product; ItemType.php:6-7 VEG=5/NON_VEG=10. Veg filter is LIVE and wired, not dead: MenuComponent.vue:10-20 has real VEG/NON_VEG toggle buttons calling itemTypeSet/itemTypeReset, also present in OffersItemComponent:81-84 and SearchItemComponent:86-89; ItemComponent.vue:3 v-show=type===item.item_type filters with prop type:Number (299). So a quick-created Chicken Burger (VEG=5) DOES surface under the 'Végétarien' menu filter -> real misclassification (advisor's DROP hypothesis refuted by this binding evidence). Stays P3: full editor shares same VEG default (ItemCreateComponent.vue:337), dietary truth carried by is_vegetarian/is_halal/allergen booleans, low V1 blast radius, pre-existing convention not a regression.

- **[P3] Dead env('DEMO') branch in ItemController::store — both arms identical** — `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ItemController.php:137-141` (deadcode, code)
  - **What** : store() wraps the create in `if (env('DEMO')) { return new ItemResource($this->itemService->store($request)); } else { return new ItemResource($this->itemService->store($request)); }` — both branches call the exact same code. The conditional is inert. Also a CLAUDE.md anti-pattern (env() read at runtime instead of config()).
  - **Why it matters** : No functional impact, but it is precisely the 'flou / unnecessary thing' the owner called out: dead conditional that misleads a reader into thinking DEMO mode changes item creation behavior (it does not), and reading env() at request time bypasses config caching.
  - **Reproduce** : Read lines 137-141; both branches are textually identical.
  - **Correction** : Replace the whole if/else with a single `return new ItemResource($this->itemService->store($request));`.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED ItemController.php:135-141: both arms of `if (env('DEMO'))` call the identical `new ItemResource($this->itemService->store($request))`. Inert dead conditional + runtime env() read (CLAUDE.md anti-pattern, bypasses config cache). Zero functional impact -> P3 cosmetic/cleanup, correctly rated. Worth the one-line simplification.

- **[P3] Item update only patches variation name+price for existing rows — item_attribute_id / visible_on silently ignored** — `app/Services/ItemService.php:326-329` (logic, code)
  - **What** : When a submitted variation carries an 'id', ItemService::update applies `->update(['name'=>..., 'price'=>...])` only (lines 326-329). Any other editable variation field present in the payload (e.g. item_attribute_id grouping, visible_on surface array, status) is dropped on the update path, even though the CREATE path (line 334 `$item->variations()->create($variation)`) persists the full row. Same caveat as S3-L1 on reachability (admin UI uses the dedicated ItemVariationController, not this blob).
  - **Why it matters** : Latent SSOT inconsistency: a bulk/API caller that re-submits an existing variation expecting to move it to a different attribute group or change its visibility surface will see only name/price take effect, with no error — a silent partial save. Confined to the same non-UI path as S3-L1 so P3.
  - **Reproduce** : PUT /api/admin/item/{id} with variations=[{id:10,name:'Grand',price:8,visible_on:['kiosk']}]. visible_on is ignored; row keeps old visible_on.
  - **Correction** : Update the full set of editable variation columns for id-bearing rows (mirror the create payload), or document/enforce that the blob path only accepts name+price.
  - **RED verdict** : CONFIRMED ItemService.php:326-329: id-bearing variation update applies only name+price; create path (334) persists the full row. ItemVariation fillable (model lines 19-26) includes item_attribute_id + visible_on (cast array, used by visibleOn() line 45) -> resubmitting an existing variation with a changed visible_on/item_attribute_id silently drops those fields with no error. Real silent partial-save. Same latent reachability as L1 (live UI uses ItemVariationController, not the blob) so P3 correctly stands; confidence was medium but evidence is solid. is_necessary true for SSOT correctness; low V1 urgency.

**Coverage gaps (RED):**
- ItemVariationController::update/store/destroy (routes/api.php:734-739) is the ACTUALLY-reachable live variation CRUD path used by the admin editor, yet NOT audited - two findings (L1,X1) excuse themselves as latent because the live path is this controller, but no finding inspects it for the same orphan/partial-save/branch-isolation bugs where a real regression would live.
- Destructive @click handlers in CatalogStudioComponent.vue unaudited: destroyItem (line 158) and destroyCategory (line 71) - no check of soft-vs-force delete semantics, branch isolation on delete, or cascade behavior on items with live composer profiles/variations.
- ItemService::store variations path (line 269 createMany) and destroy path (457-468) not adversarially examined for branch_id assignment / BranchScope leakage on multi-branch (V2-relevant; V1 single-branch low-risk).
- ComposerStepController::store/destroy (routes/api.php:783,785) dispatch dispatchProfileChanged too (ComposerStepService.php:18,27,36) - the same version-bump-on-published applies to add/remove step, not just reorder; L2 only covers the reorder path, leaving add/delete-step 409 traps unaudited.

### S4-stock-ingredients — S4-stock-ingredients: Stock + ingredients + rupture dashboard
Files examined: `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/AvailabilityController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/StockRuptureDashboardController.php`, `app/Services/Menu/AvailabilityService.php`, `app/Services/Stock/ChoiceAvailabilityResolver.php`, `app/Services/Ingredients/IngredientAvailabilityService.php`, `app/Console/Commands/StockScanRupture.php`, `app/Models/StockMovement.php`, `resources/js/components/admin/stock/StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/ingredients/IngredientAvailabilityToggleComponent.vue`, `resources/js/components/admin/dashboard/StockLowAlertsWidget.vue`, `resources/js/router/modules/stockRoutes.js`, `routes/api.php`, `config/catalog_v15.php`, `app/Console/Kernel.php`, `database/migrations/2026_04_15_230100_create_item_branch_availability_table.php`, `database/migrations/2026_04_18_130001_add_diet_flags_to_items_table.php`, `database/migrations/2026_05_05_000040_add_availability_to_item_extras_table.php`

**Confirmed corrections:**

- **[P2] Stock dashboard and customer surfaces disagree about the same ingredient: an extra/variation 86'd via the Ingredients admin still shows IN-STOCK on the Stock-Rupture dashboard** — `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/StockRuptureDashboardController.php:384-393 + 442-451; app/Services/Stock/ChoiceAvailabilityResolver.php:296-302 + 308-318; app/Services/Ingredients/IngredientAvailabilityService.php:34-62` (logic, code)
  - **What** : catalogOverview() resolves extra availability ONLY from stock_levels.manual_unavailable_reason (extraStockLevels query at L384-393 filters whereNotNull('manual_unavailable_reason'); buildGroupedPayload L543-545 sets is_available = (unavailableCount===0)). Same for variations L442-451. It NEVER reads ItemExtra.is_available nor ItemAttribute.is_available (those columns are not even SELECTed). But the customer-facing ChoiceAvailabilityResolver::availabilityForExtra (L296-302) reads extra->getRawOriginal('is_available') FIRST and returns ingredient_rupture when false; availabilityForVariation (L308-318) does the same on the attribute. IngredientAvailabilityService::toggle (L34-62) writes exactly item_extra.is_available / item_attribute.is_available cascade-by-name. So the two write paths target two different columns and the dashboard read path ignores one of them.
  - **Why it matters** : Two admin surfaces report opposite truths for the same physical ingredient. A manager who 86'd 'Sauce Algérienne' from the Ingredients page (correctly removing it from kiosk/POS) opens the Stock-Rupture dashboard and sees it green/IN-STOCK, so he believes it is sellable, may toggle it back, or trusts a wrong inventory picture during a rush. The dashboard is the operator's at-a-glance rupture board; it silently lies about every ingredient ruptured through the Ingredients admin.
  - **Reproduce** : Code-proven. (1) Ingredients admin: PUT /api/admin/ingredients/{globalId}/availability isAvailable=false -> item_extra.is_available=0 cascade-by-name. (2) GET /api/admin/stock/catalog-overview -> extra still is_available:true because its stock_levels row has no manual_unavailable_reason. (3) GET kiosk menu -> same extra correctly hidden (ChoiceAvailabilityResolver returns ingredient_rupture). Dashboard vs customer disagree.
  - **Correction** : Make catalogOverview() the single read model: when building extra/variation payloads, also factor ItemExtra.is_available / ItemAttribute.is_available (ingredient-level rupture), e.g. SELECT those columns and treat is_available=false as out-of-stock with reason 'ingredient_rupture', mirroring ChoiceAvailabilityResolver's precedence (manual ingredient flag wins, then stock_levels.manual_unavailable_reason). Alternatively unify both admin toggles onto one storage column. Either way the dashboard must read every signal the customer surfaces read.
  - **RED verdict** : VERIFIED. catalogOverview() builds extra availability ONLY from manual_unavailable_reason: extras fetched via ->get(['id','name','group_label']) (StockRuptureDashboardController.php:379) and extraStockLevels filtered whereNotNull('manual_unavailable_reason') (L391); buildGroupedPayload sets is_available = unavailableCount===0 where unavailableCount counts only ids present in that filtered set (L551-555). Variations identical: variationStockLevels also whereNotNull('manual_unavailable_reason') (L451) and the variation builder computes unavailableCount from $variationStockLevels only (L466-471) while ->with(['itemAttribute:id,name']) loads only id+name, so attribute->is_available is not even available. Customer path differs: ChoiceAvailabilityResolver::availabilityForExtra reads extra->getRawOriginal('is_available') FIRST and returns ingredient_rupture when false (L296-301), availabilityForVariation same on the attribute (L312-318). IngredientAvailabilityService::toggle writes item_extra.is_available / item_attribute.is_available cascade-by-name (L34-62). So an ingredient 86'd via the Ingredients admin (item_extra.is_available=0, no stock_levels row) shows green on the dashboard while correctly hidden on kiosk/POS. Two admin truth surfaces contradict during a rush. P1 kept: genuine operator-confusing contradiction. Calibration note: admin-only display inconsistency, no customer oversell (customer surfaces correctly hide), single-box V1 admin-only — defensibly P2, but two contradicting admin boards justify P1.
  - **Self-audit re-rating** : P1 → P2
  - **Self-audit note** : Self-audit re-rating P1->P2: same root cause as S4-02 (two divergent availability columns — stock_levels.manual_unavailable_reason vs item_extra/item_attribute.is_available). Real divergence, but align severity with its own root-cause finding.

- **[P2] Two parallel, divergent toggle systems for the SAME extras: dashboard toggle writes per-extra-id to stock_levels (no name-cascade); Ingredients admin writes cascade-by-name to item_extra — they do not converge** — `resources/js/components/admin/stock/StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue:545-565 + 604-623; app/Services/Menu/AvailabilityService.php:523-571 (toggleStockable); app/Services/Ingredients/IngredientAvailabilityService.php:41-62` (logic, code)
  - **What** : Stock dashboard toggleProduct for kind='extra' fans out one POST per extra_id to /menu/availability/extra/toggle (L545-554), which calls AvailabilityService::toggleStockable writing stock_levels.manual_unavailable_reason for that ONE (branch,type,id) tuple (L523-571) — no name cascade. The Ingredients admin instead cascades by name across every sibling ItemExtra row (IngredientAvailabilityService L50-62, deliberately, because 'Jambon de dinde' exists 32x). The dashboard groups extras by name and sends extra_ids[] for the bucket, so it covers the group it loaded — but it persists to a DIFFERENT column (stock_levels) than the Ingredients admin (item_extra.is_available). Toggling in one surface is invisible to the other.
  - **Why it matters** : Owner mandate flags 'blurs / unnecessary things'. Two storage mechanisms + two cascade strategies for one concept (extra rupture) is exactly that blur: state is split across stock_levels.manual_unavailable_reason and item_extra.is_available with no reconciliation, so the rupture truth depends on WHICH admin page the manager happened to use. It also doubles the maintenance/test surface and is the root cause of S4-01.
  - **Reproduce** : Code-proven by tracing both write paths to different columns; observable as S4-01's symptom.
  - **Correction** : Pick ONE SSOT for extra/variation rupture. Recommended: route the Stock dashboard's extra/variation toggles through IngredientAvailabilityService (cascade-by-name on item_extra.is_available) so both admin surfaces and ChoiceAvailabilityResolver agree, and reserve stock_levels.manual_unavailable_reason for true per-branch on-hand rupture. Document the chosen SSOT in CLAUDE.md to prevent re-divergence.
  - **RED verdict** : VERIFIED. Two divergent write mechanisms for the same 'extra rupture' concept confirmed. Dashboard toggleProduct kind='extra' fans out one POST per extra_id to admin/menu/availability/extra/toggle (StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue:547-554) -> AvailabilityService::toggleStockable writes stock_levels.manual_unavailable_reason for one (branch,type,id) tuple with NO name cascade (AvailabilityService.php:524-571). Ingredients admin instead cascades by name across sibling ItemExtra rows on item_extra.is_available (IngredientAvailabilityService.php:41-62, name+group_label scoped). State split across two columns with no reconciliation; this is the root cause of S4-01. P2 correct: real architectural blur, not a customer-facing fault. Necessary to unify per owner anti-blur mandate, but lower urgency than the visible contradiction in S4-01.

- **[P3] Entire daily-quota auto-86 subsystem is unreachable in V1: max_daily_qty is never set by any UI, seeder, or factory, so setMaxDailyQty endpoint + decrementForOrder auto-86 + releaseForOrderItems auto-restore are all dormant** — `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/AvailabilityController.php:111-147 (setMaxDailyQty); app/Services/Menu/AvailabilityService.php:99-166 + 286-381 + 710-750; database/migrations/2026_04_15_230100_create_item_branch_availability_table.php:22; routes/api.php:305-306` (deadcode, code)
  - **What** : max_daily_qty is nullable with NO DB default (migration L22) and grep across database/migrations + seeders + factories + app shows it is only WRITTEN by AvailabilityService::setMaxDailyQty, which is reachable only via POST /menu/availability/max-daily-qty (routes/api.php:305). grep of resources/js for 'max-daily-qty|max_daily_qty|setMaxDailyQty' returns ZERO callers — the V2 dashboard rewrite dropped the cap UI. Therefore max_daily_qty stays NULL forever, so: decrementForOrder's flip (L347 whereNotNull('max_daily_qty')) never fires, the out_of_stock auto-86 (L360-379) never fires, and releaseForOrderItems auto-restore (L727-744, gated on max_daily_qty !== null) never fires.
  - **Why it matters** : Large, intricate, locked-and-after-commit-dispatched logic (quota counters, CAS flip, per-order Cache::add idempotency guard, reset cron, CatalogWarningService 85% warnings) that cannot execute on a real Le Cayenne shift because no operator can ever set a cap. It is dead weight that misleads future readers into believing auto-86-by-quota works, and it inflates the test/maintenance surface — precisely the 'unnecessary things' the owner called out.
  - **Reproduce** : grep -rn 'max-daily-qty|max_daily_qty|setMaxDailyQty' resources/js -> no result. grep max_daily_qty in seeders/factories -> none set it. Column nullable, no default.
  - **Correction** : Decide explicitly: either (a) re-expose a per-item daily-cap input in the Stock dashboard so the subsystem is reachable, or (b) mark the quota path as V1.x-deferred and remove/quarantine setMaxDailyQty route + the decrement/restore quota branches to shrink the surface. Do not leave it half-wired. Note: NF525 not affected (daily_consumed_qty is a UX counter, not fiscal).
  - **RED verdict** : VERIFIED but DOWNGRADED P2->P3. Dead-code claim is accurate: max_daily_qty is nullable with no DB default (migration L22), grep of resources/js for max-daily-qty|max_daily_qty|setMaxDailyQty|maxDailyQty returns ZERO callers, and grep of database/ shows max_daily_qty only in the migration (no seeder/factory writes it). So setMaxDailyQty route (routes/api.php:305) is UI-orphaned, decrementForOrder's quota flip is gated whereNotNull('max_daily_qty') (AvailabilityService.php:347) and the out_of_stock auto-86 (L360-379) never fire, and releaseForOrderItems auto-restore (L727-744, gated max_daily_qty !== null) never fires. Code is real and correct. BUT severity is over-rated: dormant, well-gated, fiscally inert (daily_consumed_qty is a UX counter not NF525), causes no wrong behavior in V1 — it is latent surface, not a defect. P3 'decide+quarantine-or-expose' is the right weight, not P2.

- **[P3] Rupture-scan 'run' endpoint, last-summary endpoint and the whole preventive cron have no UI trigger and the cron ships disabled by default** — `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/StockRuptureDashboardController.php:207-239 (run) + 50-85 (lastSummary); config/catalog_v15.php:137; app/Console/Kernel.php:253-255; resources/js (no caller)` (deadcode, code)
  - **What** : POST /api/admin/stock/scan-rupture/run and GET /stock/scan-rupture/last-summary exist (routes/api.php:313-324) and the controller is fully implemented, but grep of resources/js for 'scan-rupture/run', 'scan-rupture/last-summary', 'lastSummary', 'scanRupture' returns ZERO callers — the V2 dashboard (StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue) only calls catalog-overview + the 3 toggles. The automatic cron exists (Kernel L253) but is gated by config('catalog_v15.auto_86_preventive_cron.enabled') which defaults to false (config/catalog_v15.php:137 env FK_CATALOG_AUTO_86_CRON_ENABLED default false), and StockScanRupture::handle() L58-61 early-returns when disabled.
  - **Why it matters** : The 'rupture scan run' capability the operator would expect from a Stock-Rupture dashboard is not invocable from the UI at all, and the only automated path is off by default. So preventive auto-86 (the feature's reason to exist: stop selling an item whose every variation is exhausted between orders) does nothing on a default V1 install. An operator cannot manually trigger a scan either. This is a functional gap masquerading as a built feature.
  - **Reproduce** : grep -rn 'scan-rupture' resources/js -> only css/route mentions, no axios call. config default enabled=false. Kernel command guarded by same flag.
  - **Correction** : If preventive auto-86 is desired for V1, enable the cron (set FK_CATALOG_AUTO_86_CRON_ENABLED=true) and/or add a 'Lancer le scan' button in the dashboard wired to POST /stock/scan-rupture/run with dry-run preview. If not desired for V1, mark run/last-summary/cron as deferred so they are not mistaken for live features.
  - **RED verdict** : VERIFIED, DOWNGRADED P2->P3, and STRENGTHENED on one point. run() (StockRuptureDashboardController.php:207-239) and lastSummary (L50-85) exist and are fully implemented; routes present (routes/api.php:313,323). grep resources/js for scan-rupture|lastSummary|scanRupture|last-summary returns ZERO axios callers (the Vue dashboard only calls catalog-overview + the 3 toggles + low-alerts). Cron is gated by config('catalog_v15.auto_86_preventive_cron.enabled') which env-defaults false (config/catalog_v15.php:137) and Kernel.php:258 ->when(...) honors it. EXTRA finding: even run()'s Artisan::call('stock:scan-rupture') would no-op because StockScanRupture::handle() early-returns when the same flag is false (StockScanRupture.php:58-61) — so wiring a button alone wouldn't help without also flipping the flag. Real functional gap but severity over-rated: preventive auto-86 is a convenience, V1 single-box operator can 86 manually via the live toggles; nothing breaks. P3.

- **[P3] Dashboard extra/variation toggle is non-atomic: a partial fan-out failure rolls back the UI flip but leaves some extra_ids already persisted out-of-stock in the DB** — `resources/js/components/admin/stock/StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue:604-623 (sendBulkToggle) + 567-575 (catch rollback)` (logic, code)
  - **What** : sendBulkToggle fans out N independent POSTs in batches of 2 with Promise.allSettled (L610-621); it records firstError and throws AFTER the whole loop (L622). Each successful POST has ALREADY committed its own stock_levels row server-side (toggleStockable is per-id, no group transaction). toggleProduct's catch (L567-570) only reverts the single optimistic product.is_available in the Vue state — it does NOT send compensating re-toggles for the ids that already succeeded. Comment at L601-602 claims 'all-or-nothing semantics' but the implementation is best-effort-with-UI-rollback, not transactional.
  - **Why it matters** : For a deduped bucket backed by many sibling extra_ids (e.g. a sauce present in 30 products), if the 7th POST 429s or 500s, the first 6 ids are persisted unavailable in stock_levels while the dashboard shows the product back as IN-STOCK after rollback. The DB and the screen now disagree, and the partially-86'd ids silently affect customer surfaces. The 'all-or-nothing' docstring gives false confidence.
  - **Reproduce** : needs-live-verify: toggle a multi-id bucket, force one POST to fail (throttle/offline mid-batch); observe UI reverts to in-stock while GET catalog-overview shows any_unavailable_count>0 for that product.
  - **Correction** : Either make the toggle truly atomic server-side (one endpoint accepting extra_ids[] wrapped in a DB transaction) or, on partial failure, fire compensating re-toggles for the ids that already succeeded before reverting the UI, and surface a precise error. At minimum correct the misleading 'all-or-nothing' docstring.
  - **RED verdict** : VERIFIED, DOWNGRADED P2->P3. sendBulkToggle fans out N independent POSTs in batches of 2 via Promise.allSettled, records firstError, throws AFTER the full loop (StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue:618-635); each toggleStockable commits its own stock_levels row in its own DB.transaction per id (AvailabilityService.php:525) with no group transaction; toggleProduct catch only reverts the single optimistic product.is_available (L566-568) and sends no compensating re-toggles. The 'All-or-nothing semantics' docstring (L601-602) is misleading — implementation is best-effort with UI-only rollback. Real partial-write divergence. BUT severity over-rated: requires a mid-batch network failure on a multi-id bucket; blast radius is a small partial-86 that errs SAFE (some siblings unavailable) and is self-healing on next full toggle; confidence is correctly 'medium' (live-verify). The most-defensible concrete fix is the cheap docstring correction. P3.

- **[P3] Dashboard sends reason 'out_of_stock_manual' but stock_levels.unavailable_reason column is 64-char and downstream reasons are inconsistent ('out_of_stock' vs 'out_of_stock_manual' vs 'stock_rupture') — no canonical reason vocabulary** — `resources/js/components/admin/stock/StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue:248 (DEFAULT_REASON='out_of_stock_manual'); app/Services/Menu/AvailabilityService.php:141 ('out_of_stock') + 742 ('released_after_cancel_or_refund'); app/Console/Commands/StockScanRupture.php:130 ('stock_rupture'); app/Http/Controllers/Admin/StockRuptureDashboardController.php:59 (filters where unavailable_reason='stock_rupture')` (logic, code)
  - **What** : The same logical state 'item is 86' is written with at least 4 distinct reason strings depending on path: dashboard manual toggle -> 'out_of_stock_manual'; quota auto-86 -> 'out_of_stock'; preventive cron -> 'stock_rupture'; release -> 'released_after_cancel_or_refund'. lastSummary's currently_unavailable query (StockRuptureDashboardController L59) hard-filters where('unavailable_reason','stock_rupture'), so an item 86'd manually via the dashboard ('out_of_stock_manual') or by quota ('out_of_stock') is EXCLUDED from that 'currently unavailable' list.
  - **Why it matters** : Reason strings are free-form and unaligned, so any report or filter keyed on a specific reason silently omits items 86'd by another path (concretely, lastSummary under-reports). It also means the operator-facing 'why' is inconsistent. This is a textbook 'blur' the owner flagged: no enum, no shared constant, magic strings scattered across JS, service and command.
  - **Reproduce** : Code-proven: grep the four literals; observe lastSummary L59 filter excludes the other reasons. (lastSummary itself is currently UI-orphaned per S4-04, but the divergent vocabulary is the root issue.)
  - **Correction** : Introduce a single UnavailableReason enum/const shared by JS (i18n-mapped) and PHP; normalize the dashboard manual reason and the lastSummary filter to the same canonical set, or have lastSummary include all manual/auto reasons rather than only 'stock_rupture'.
  - **RED verdict** : VERIFIED, DOWNGRADED P2->P3. Four distinct reason strings for '86' confirmed: dashboard manual -> 'out_of_stock_manual' (StockRuptureDashboardComponent.vue:248); quota auto-86 -> 'out_of_stock' (AvailabilityService.php:143,360); preventive cron -> 'stock_rupture' (StockScanRupture.php:130); release -> 'released_after_cancel_or_refund' (AvailabilityService.php:742). lastSummary hard-filters where('unavailable_reason','stock_rupture') (StockRuptureDashboardController.php:58), excluding manual/quota 86s. No shared enum; magic strings scattered. BUT severity over-rated: the only concrete observable consumer (lastSummary) is itself UI-orphaned per S4-04, so the under-report harms nobody in V1; the rest is a maintainability/consistency blur, not a behavioral defect. P3, contingent (becomes more relevant if lastSummary is ever wired).

**Dropped by adversarial pass:**
- ~~[P3] releaseForOrderItems auto-restore ignores the original unavailable_reason: a quota-flip restore re-enables an item even if it was independently flagged for another reason while a cap was set~~ — VERIFIED real inconsistency but DROP for V1. releaseForOrderItems shouldFlip checks only wasUnavailable && max_daily_qty !== null && newConsumed < max_daily_qty (AvailabilityService.php:726-729) and does NOT gate on unavailable_reason === 'out_of_stock', whereas setMaxDailyQty's auto-restore DOES gate previousReason === 'out_of_stock' (L150). So a refund could resurrect a manually-86'd item IF a cap were set. The asymmetry is genuine and the recommended one-line gate is correct. HOWEVER it is is_necessary:false for V1: it is doubly-dormant — gated by max_daily_qty !== null which S4-03 proves is ALWAYS null (no UI/seeder/factory ever sets a cap), so shouldFlip can never be true. Zero reachable wrong-result in V1. The auditor itself rates P3 'dormant'. As a standalone fix with no reachable trigger, DROP (or fold into the S4-03 quota decision); only becomes live (then P1) if/when the quota subsystem from S4-03 is enabled.
